- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Players lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        - Rationale: India 97% to victory in 2 turns. Zero chattiness to avoid any diplomatic delays. Maximum aggression maintained. No deception - enemies know I'm coming. Total focus on war declaration and strike execution before turn 354 when India becomes Influential.

        ## Relationships
        
        ### Arabia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Arabia reduced to vassal status under Iroquois, only Damascus remains, and peace treaty is active. Reset relationship to neutral - they're no longer a threat and our ally The Iroquois controls them. No need to maintain hostility.
        - UpdatedTurn: 239
        
        ### Siam
        - Public: 30
        - Private: 30
        - Rationale: Siam remains friendly with us and fighting Rome. They have 3575 military strength and a defensive pact with The Iroquois. With The Iroquois about to fall under Indian influence, Siam is our secondary line of defense. They have Open Borders with India, but maintaining positive relations keeps them from actively joining an anti-Portuguese coalition. Their military strength combined with ours provides deterrence.
        - UpdatedTurn: 325
        
        ### Byzantium
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: Byzantium has Defensive Pact with India and is under Indian cultural influence. They are a direct threat that could attack when I DOW India. Maintaining negative relations to prepare for potential betrayal. Cannot trust them.
        - UpdatedTurn: 344
        
        ### Carthage
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Carthage is defeated with only Hippo Regius remaining. Reset to neutral to prepare for peace treaty and free up diplomatic focus for India war. No need to maintain hostility against defeated foe.
        - UpdatedTurn: 352
        
        ### India
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: INDIA: 97% POPULAR, 2 TURNS FROM INFLUENTIAL CULTURAL VICTORY. Total war is now the only option. Must declare war immediately and attempt nuclear strike or conventional assault with paratroopers/bombers. Maintaining -100/-100 relations to prepare for immediate DOW.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### Rome
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: War with Rome is now a distraction from the existential India threat. Resetting to neutral to free forces and prepare for potential last-ditch strike against India, though they will likely win cultural victory next turn regardless.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 90
        - Rationale: The Iroquois: Now have Open Borders with India and are under Indian cultural influence (Influential status). They could flip to India's side when war starts. Maintaining 90/90 to delay betrayal. If they join India, I'm surrounded by enemies. The defensive pact is the only thing keeping them neutral.
        - UpdatedTurn: 353
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Atomic Theory research continuing (14 turns). No policy available. Maintaining current strategic posture as India will win cultural victory next turn regardless of actions. Maximum war readiness in case of miracle.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - Open Door: Ur:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 328
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 294
          - World Religion: Hinduism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Endowment for the Arts:
            - Description: Increases up to 33% generation of Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians. Decreases up to 33% generation of Great Scientists, Engineers, and Merchants.
              Civilizations with Scores from Policies and Great Works below the global median will receive a boost to Production and Culture up to 33%.
              Details: The boosts are proportional to the difference between the score of the Civilization and the leader.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Rome:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 278
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - Proposer: India
          - Enact: United Nations:
            - Description: Begins the United Nations project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : United Nations wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World. Also, when you expend a Great Person, earn 25 Influence with all City-States you have met. Triggers a Golden Age, as well as the United Nations Special Session of the World Congress if the world has reached the Atomic Era.)
              : Receive an immediate boost of 25 Influence with all City-States. The Consulate National Wonder appears in Capital. (Receive 1 Additional Delegate in the World Congress for every 8 City-States originally in the World.)
              : Receive a free Great Diplomat.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - Proposer: Siam
          - Enact: Byzantine Sphere of Influence: Singapore:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: Byzantium

        You, Maria I (leader of Portugal, Player 2), are making strategic decisions after turn 354.
- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Maria I, leader of Portugal (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Portugal
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Nau, Replacing Caravel (Requires technology: Compass)
        - Unique Unit: Caçador, Replacing Gatling Gun (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: University of Coimbra, Replacing Imperial College (Requires technology: Education)
        - Unique Improvement: Feitoria (Can be constructed by Workers in owned land to provide yields or by Portuguese-owned Naus (via Sell Exotic Goods) in City-State land.
          In owned land:
          Can only be built on coastal tiles, and cannot be adjacent to another Feitoria.
          In City-State land:
          Provides an untradeable copy of each Luxury Resource type owned by the City-State. Trade Routes to this City-State generate bonus Food and Production based on Gold generated from the Trade Route and relationship with the City-State (i.e. Neutral/Friend/Ally).
          In addition to these bonuses, it provides vision within a 2-tile radius and a +25% Defensive Strength bonus for any Unit stationed in this tile. Contains a Canal, allowing friendly Sea Units to traverse through this tile.); Requires technology: Compass
        - Civilization Ability: For each active Trade Route, receive +4 Gold, Science, and Great Admiral () or Great General () Points, scaling with Era.
      - Leader: Maria I
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      - Refrigeration: Allows Work Boats to construct an Offshore Platform, and increases Trade Route maximum travel distance.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics, Computers
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      
      ## Policies
      - Empiricism (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * +1 Food for every Citizen and +25% Growth in all Cities.
        - * +3% Science in a City for every Great Work present (up to 20%).
      - Creative Expression (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Creative Expression: +2 Tourism from Great Works. Museums, Broadcast Towers, Opera Houses, and Amphitheaters gain +3 Golden Age Points and Culture.
      - Civil Society (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Civil Society: Specialists consume 2 Food less than normal (minimum 1 Food). Farms, Plantations, Camps, and all Unique Improvements produce +4 Food.
      - Economic Union (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Economic Union: Receive two additional Trade Routes. +6 Gold from trade routes with civilizations following Freedom.
      - Avant Garde (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Avant Garde: The rate at which Great People are born is increased by 33%. -2 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Covert Action (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Freedom): Covert Action: Receive 100 Spy Points. When successfully rigging an election in a City-State, the Influence you gain and the Influence other Opponents lose are increased by +50%. Spies stationed in a foreign City gain +10 Network Points per turn.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Tribute (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * A Settler appears near the Capital.
        - * Gain Culture in Capital equal to 25% of the Gold Tribute demanded from City-States (50% if all Authority policies are adopted).
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Civilizing Mission (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Retain all buildings from conquered Cities.
        - * Receive 75 Gold when you conquer a city, scaling with Era and City Population.
        - * +10% Production toward Buildings, with an additional +10% Production per Era difference between your current Era and the building's Era in all Puppeted Cities and Cities with a Courthouse.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

Adjusting flavors to maximize Science, Production, Great People, and Espionage, while maintaining a strong Offense to capture Rome's capital and finish off Carthage, and high Air/Naval flavors to leverage our newly built bombers and protect our coastal trade network.
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Policy: Economic Union
        - Rationale: Economic Union provides 2 additional Trade Routes. Since Portugal's UA grants massive Gold, Science, and Great Person points for every active Trade Route, this policy is extremely synergistic, directly boosting our science output and gold generation to help us catch up with India.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Rationale: Refrigeration increases Trade Route maximum travel distance, allowing us to establish more profitable international trade routes to trigger our Unique Ability. It also unlocks Offshore Platforms for extra coastal production and is on the path to Penicillin and advanced modern technologies.
        - Technology: Refrigeration
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Public: -50
        - TargetID: 1
        - Rationale: Setting Rome's relationship to -50. We want our staff to aggressively capture Antium and Rome's capital (Rome) to secure powerful wonders and eliminate their threat, but we must NOT completely eliminate Rome from the game, as doing so would remove the only buffer preventing India from winning an instant Cultural Victory.
        - Private: -50

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Assessing Victory Progress**
            
            My current focus is on evaluating India's victory trajectory. Specifically, I'm reviewing their cultural victory progress, which was nearing completion last turn and could potentially be achieved by today.
            
            **Evaluating Victory Progress**
            
            My current analysis indicates India is no longer on the verge of immediate cultural victory. Rome's continued influence at 68% and 41 turns to influential status has provided a crucial buffer. I'm now shifting focus to our own victory conditions and strategic options.
            
            **Exploring Policy Options**
            
            I'm currently assessing our policy choices. It appears we didn't select one last turn due to insufficient culture, and we still require 29 turns before another policy choice is available. In parallel, I'm reviewing available technologies for future research, considering Ballistics, Refrigeration, and Penicillin.
            
            **Analyzing Global Conflict**
            
            My thoughts are now consumed by the global war Rome is embroiled in. With Rome warring against nearly every major civilization, their potential to curb India's cultural victory is severely diminished. This presents a critical opportunity for us to re-evaluate our own strategic position and capitalize on the unfolding geopolitical landscape.
            
            **Assessing Threat Vectors**
            
            I'm now focused on India's imminent victory potential. They possess the delegates for a Diplomatic Victory in seven turns and have ample gold to secure city-states, while also maintaining strong military and peaceful relations. Our current military strength is insufficient to directly challenge them.
            
            **Revising Diplomatic Alliances**
            
            I'm re-examining our defensive pacts. It appears I overlooked that India has no listed defensive pacts, which significantly alters my understanding of potential conflicts. This means my assumption about the Iroquois automatically joining an offensive war against India was incorrect.
            
            **Revising Unit Acquisitions**
            
            I'm reviewing our recent unit acquisitions. It appears I misread previous production logs; we actually purchased bombers and a triplane with gold last turn. The significant gold influx from the Iroquois deal explains this expenditure, which I'm now factoring into our current financial standing.
            
            **Capturing Strategic Capitals**
            
            I'm re-evaluating our military objectives given the recent massive gold influx. We used the 5133 gold from the Plastics trade to acquire Triplanes and Bombers, bringing our current gold to 774 with a +121 income per turn. We've successfully captured Carthage and Utique, and now hold three capitals out of eight, positioning us to potentially capture Rome next and advance our domination victory path. I'm also considering the cultural implications for India if Rome's cities are annexed.
            
            **Preventing Cultural Victory**
            
            I've realized that eliminating Rome would actually grant India an instant cultural victory by reducing the number of civilizations they need to influence. My immediate priority is to prevent Rome's elimination to avoid this outcome.
            
            **Preventing Instant Loss**
            
            I'm realizing that eliminating Rome would trigger India's cultural victory. My immediate focus is on preventing Rome's complete annihilation. I'm exploring if we can capture Rome's capital while leaving them enough cities to survive, thus avoiding the victory condition, but I must also consider the risk of other warring nations eliminating them.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm currently reassessing India's path to victory. The Passport System proposal at the World Congress is highly concerning, as it would accelerate their tourism, but Siam's United Nations proposal is even more critical. If enacted, it triggers World Leader elections, and India's extensive city-state alliances and gold reserves make a Diplomatic Victory almost certain. My focus is now on preventing this specific outcome.
            
            **Disrupting Diplomatic Victory**
            
            My current focus is disrupting India's imminent diplomatic victory. The United Nations proposal is the most critical threat; I'll vote against it if possible. I'm also targeting India's allied city-states with spies and considering aggressive gold acquisition to buy influence, all while assessing the impact of a direct declaration of war.
            
            **Assessing Diplomatic Maneuvers**
            
            I'm now assessing potential diplomatic and military actions against India. My immediate concern is India's distant cities and the feasibility of reaching them with current air units, considering their limited range and the lack of nearby bases or friendly city-states. I'm also re-evaluating our defensive pacts in light of this.
            
            **Researching Flight Tech**
            
            I am now researching Flight technology to enable bomber re-basing. I must reach Byzantium's cities to potentially rebase our air units, as our current diplomatic status with them is confusingly "Friendly" despite a low relationship score. Carrier technology is not yet available.
            
            **Prioritizing Science**
            
            I'm now prioritizing research towards Nuclear Fission. My previous research options were limited because Atomic Theory wasn't yet complete. With 1 turn remaining until Atomic Theory is done, Nuclear Fission will unlock, which is key for future strategic goals. I'm also considering the economic benefits of the "Economic Union" policy for Portugal.
            
            **Adopting Policy Strategies**
            
            I am now evaluating policy choices, prioritizing "Economic Union" for Portugal due to its synergy with our UA and trade route potential. I'm also reassessing our overall Grand Strategy, weighing a continued Conquest path against a potential shift towards a Science Victory, given our strong science output and technological proximity to India.
            
            **Preventing Instant Loss**
            
            I'm realizing that eliminating Rome would trigger India's cultural victory. My immediate focus is on preventing Rome's complete annihilation. I'm exploring if we can capture Rome's capital while leaving them enough cities to survive, thus avoiding the victory condition, but I must also consider the risk of other warring nations eliminating them.
            
            **Securing Capitals Strategically**
            
            I'm now confirming the survival of Rome after capturing their capital. With Antium and Rome secured, they will retain sufficient cities to avoid triggering India's victory. I'm evaluating the military assets at Antium, including paratroopers and a rifleman, and assessing our bomber capabilities across different zones, noting recent gold-driven acquisitions in Porto.
            
            **Assessing Bomber Range**
            
            I'm re-checking bomber reach from Utique and Carthage to Antium. My previous calculations were off; Antium is very close to both locations, well within bomber range. This allows for effective support of ground units.
            
            **Disrupting Diplomatic Victory**
            
            I'm currently assessing my options to hinder India's diplomatic victory. I'll vote against the Passport System and the Byzantine Sphere of Influence proposal, aiming to disrupt their progress. My spy in Venezia is attempting a coup, hoping to shift their allegiance from India.
            
            **Assessing Declarations of War**
            
            I'm weighing the advantages of declaring war on India. Plundering their trade routes would yield substantial gold, and ending open borders would reduce their tourism impact. However, their influence with us is already at maximum, making this a nuanced decision with potential negative diplomatic repercussions.
            
            **Evaluating Immediate Threats**
            
            I'm currently reassessing the risks of a declaration of war on India. Given their superior military strength and our ongoing conflicts, a direct confrontation could be catastrophic, potentially leading to the loss of core cities. I'm prioritizing securing our existing gains and advancing our technology instead.
            
            **Shifting Grand Strategy**
            
            I am now evaluating a shift towards a Science Victory. My focus is on optimizing Grand Strategy settings to prioritize science, production, and air power, while maintaining sufficient defensive and offensive capabilities. I will also adjust flavors for naval, air, and nuclear deterrence.
            
            **Adopting Spaceship Strategy**
            
            I'm now fully committed to a Science Victory path, optimizing Grand Strategy for science, food, and production. I'll research Refrigeration next, as it boosts trade routes and unlocks crucial technologies. "Economic Union" policy is my priority to amplify Portugal's UA.
            
            **Adjusting Diplomatic Postures**
            
            I am now reviewing our relationships with other civilizations. Rome's relationship is being adjusted to -50/-50 to ensure aggressive prosecution of our war while preventing their complete annihilation. Carthage is nearly eliminated, so their relationship status is a lower priority. India remains our primary competitor, with a relationship of -100/-100.
            
            **Refining Diplomatic Maneuvers**
            
            I'm adjusting our stance with Siam, maintaining our current Diplomatic Favor and Open Borders status. I will then proceed to set our Grand Strategy towards a Spaceship victory, focusing on Science, Production, Offense, and Espionage. "Economic Union" will be adopted as our next policy, and Refrigeration will be researched. Rome's relationship will be modified to facilitate aggressive prosecution without their annihilation.
            
            **Optimizing Spaceship Trajectory**
            
            I am now solidifying our commitment to a Science Victory, refining Grand Strategy to heavily favor Science, Production, Espionage, and Air power. I will be adopting the "Economic Union" policy and researching Refrigeration to accelerate our spaceship component production and bolster trade routes. Rome's relationship will be managed to allow aggressive pursuit without their total elimination.
            
            **Adjusting Rome Relationship**
            
            I am now adjusting Rome's relationship to a private -50 and public -50. This strategic shift is crucial to aggressively capture key cities and wonders while ensuring Rome's survival, thereby preventing India's instant cultural victory.

          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: hUGhE7Rg3B4gvnLo
          - toolName: set-policy
          - input:
            - Policy: Economic Union
            - Rationale: Economic Union provides 2 additional Trade Routes. Since Portugal's UA grants massive Gold, Science, and Great Person points for every active Trade Route, this policy is extremely synergistic, directly boosting our science output and gold generation to help us catch up with India.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: UFCrOu4O7XWd2YEm
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Rationale: Refrigeration increases Trade Route maximum travel distance, allowing us to establish more profitable international trade routes to trigger our Unique Ability. It also unlocks Offshore Platforms for extra coastal production and is on the path to Penicillin and advanced modern technologies.
            - Technology: Refrigeration
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #4:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: cGyQhD5VSTxk3nIu
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Public: -50
            - TargetID: 1
            - Rationale: Setting Rome's relationship to -50. We want our staff to aggressively capture Antium and Rome's capital (Rome) to secure powerful wonders and eliminate their threat, but we must NOT completely eliminate Rome from the game, as doing so would remove the only buffer preventing India from winning an instant Cultural Victory.
            - Private: -50
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
